Q1. Digit sum \(13\) with tens digit \(6\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(68\)
B. \(66\)
C. \(69\)
D. \(67\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
6+u=13\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 67.

\[
N=10\times 6+7=67
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 67 is 13.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(67\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=67
\]

Fast result: \(67\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
Situation: largest-digit-number
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

---
Q2. For the number \(480\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(480)=(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=24
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 24.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_prime_composite_deduction
Situation: sum-divisor-geometric
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: elite

Q3. Two buses leave a stand every \(56\) and \(104\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(692\)
B. \(728\)
C. \(764\)
D. \(801\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
56=2^{3}\times 7
\]

\[
104=2^{3}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=728
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(728\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=\frac{56\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(56,104)}=728
\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_three_number_hcf_lcm
Situation: three-number-alignment
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q4. When \(2^{25}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(25-1)\bmod 12+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{25}\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(25-1)\bmod 12+1=1
\]

\[
2^{25}\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_remainder_after_power
Situation: counter-cycle
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q5. When \(3\) is raised to \(108\), what two digits appear at the end?
A. \(63\)
B. \(61\)
C. \(62\)
D. \(60\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{100}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 20.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 27\rightarrow 81\rightarrow 43\rightarrow 29\rightarrow 87\rightarrow 61\rightarrow 83\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 47\rightarrow 41\rightarrow 23\rightarrow 69\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 21\rightarrow 63\rightarrow 89\rightarrow 67\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(108-1)\bmod 20+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 61.

\[
3^{108}\equiv 61\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 61.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(61\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(108-1)\bmod 20+1=8
\]

\[
3^{108}\equiv 61\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(61\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(57-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
7^{57}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

\[

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q8. The number \(3x561\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=3
\]

\[
3-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
3-x=0
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
3-3=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: hidden-divisor-deduction
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q14. Between \(112\) and \(290\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(24\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(25\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 112 is 112.

\[
8\times 14=112
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 290.

\[
290=8\times 36+2
\]

\[
290-2=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{288-112}{8}+1=22+1=23
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 23.
Hence, there are 23 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{290}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{111}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-13=23
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: smallest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

---

Q16. For the number \(480\), find the count of odd divisors?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

\[
O=4
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(1+1)\times (1+1)=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Situation: hidden-factor-reconstruction

Q23. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
Situation: square-divisor-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q26. The number \(41\) is written in decimal form. How many digits does it contain?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:

Q28. For \(19^{116}\), use the cycle modulo \(17\). What remainder is left?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(116-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 16.

\[
19^{116}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 16.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(116-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

\[
2^{116}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Fast result: \(504\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q36. Between \(129\) and \(254\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 129 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 240 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 254.

\[
254=15\times 16+14
\]

\[
254-14=240
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{240-135}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{254}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{128}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-8=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(842\)
D. \(727\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
45=3^{2}\times 5
\]

\[
153=3^{2}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(45,153)=765
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 765.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(765\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(45,153)=\frac{45\times 153}{\operatorname{HCF}(45,153)}=765
\]

Fast result: \(765\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q52. Power \(13^{82}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(82-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
13^{82}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(82-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

\[
2^{82}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_range_count
Situation: range-remainder-count
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 60! is 56.

Final Answer

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[

Fast result: \(56\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q56. After prime factorising \(12\), what is the number of positive divisors?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q57. For the number \(480\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(1814\)
B. \(1361\)
C. \(1512\)
D. \(1663\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 50! = 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q63. Between \(202\) and \(370\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(22\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(23\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 202 is 208.

\[
8\times 26=208
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 368 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 370.

\[
370=8\times 46+2
\]

\[
370-2=368
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{368-208}{8}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{370}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{201}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
46-25=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Situation: hidden-divisor-deduction
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{288-176}{8}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{288}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{171}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-21=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Topology depth: 3

Q78. Power \(11^{69}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
11^{69}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 12+1=9
\]

\[
11^{69}\equiv 8\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Q102. The number \(107x3\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[
11-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
11-x=11
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11-0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q103. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(11085466\)
B. \(12597120\)
C. \(15116544\)
D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(42,42)=42
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 42.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(42\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(42,42)=\frac{42\times 42}{\operatorname{HCF}(42,42)}=42
\]

Fast result: \(42\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q108. The number \(914x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: smallest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q109. After prime factorising \(108\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?

B. \(308\)
C. \(266\)
D. \(280\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(108)=280
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 280.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q112. Digits \(8\) and \(7\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+7=87
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+8=78
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-7|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\]

Final Answer

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 222 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 327.

\[
327=15\times 21+12
\]

\[
327-12=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-225}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{327}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{221}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-14=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: largest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: punjab_state

Q129. Power \(2^{36}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(36-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{36}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(36-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

\[
2^{36}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Q145. The number \(970x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: largest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: punjab_state

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=12\Rightarrow u=5
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 75.

\[
N=10\times 7+5=75
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 75 is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(75\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=5
\]

\[
N=75

Fast result: \(75\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q150. When \(3^{51}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(51-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{51}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

\[
3^{51}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q151. The number \(4x214\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 11; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
11+x=18
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
11+7=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: simultaneous-divisibility
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

Q152. After prime factorising \(24\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(364954\)
B. \(414720\)
C. \(497664\)
D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
Situation: exact-divisor-target
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

B. \(491\)
C. \(515\)
D. \(445\)
Answer: A

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,117)=468
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 468.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(468\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,117)=\frac{36\times 117}{\operatorname{HCF}(36,117)}=468
\]

Fast result: \(468\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q155. Number \(82\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(56\)
B. \(54\)
C. \(55\)
D. \(53\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+2=82
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+8=28
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-2|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-2|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Situation: digit-interchange
Topology depth: 3

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
30+15+7+3+1=56
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 60! is 56.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(56\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(56\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
8+1=9
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q175. Two buses leave a stand every \(126\) and \(234\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(1474\)
B. \(1638\)
C. \(1802\)
D. \(1966\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
126=2\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
234=2\times 3^{2}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=1638
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1638.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1638\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=\frac{126\times 234}{\operatorname{HCF}(126,234)}=1638
\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q176. Between \(230\) and \(379\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(24\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(25\)
D. \(26\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 230 is 234.

\[
6\times 39=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 378 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 379.

\[
379=6\times 63+1
\]

\[
379-1=378
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{378-234}{6}+1=24+1=25
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 25.
Hence, there are 25 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(25\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{379}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{229}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
63-38=25
\]

Fast result: \(25\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Situation: checksum-number
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

Q178. Power \(13^{42}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(42-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{42}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(42-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
4^{42}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_power
Situation: modular-reconstruction
Topology depth: 5
Exam mode: elite

D. \(78\)
Answer: A

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 76.

\[
N=10\times 7+6=76
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 76 is 13.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(76\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=6
\]

\[
N=76
\]

Fast result: \(76\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q183. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q186. Number \(62\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(38\)
B. \(36\)
C. \(37\)
D. \(35\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+2=62
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+6=26
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-2|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-2|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Q199. Between \(200\) and \(284\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 200 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 284.

\[
284=15\times 18+14
\]

\[
284-14=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-210}{15}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{284}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{199}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-13=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: checksum-number
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

Q201. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q202. Two cyclic events repeat every \(104\) and \(56\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(764\)
B. \(801\)

D. \(728\)
Answer: D

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(104,56)=728
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(728\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(104,56)=\frac{104\times 56}{\operatorname{HCF}(104,56)}=728
\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q203. Power \(7^{23}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(23-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
7^{23}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(23-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{23}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Hence, remainder = 11.

\]

Final Answer

\]

Q210. Tens digit \(3\) and ones digit \(4\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+4=34
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+3=43
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-4|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-4|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q218. The number \(407x3\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=14
\]

\[
14-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
14-x=11
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
14-3=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q223. Power \(13^{48}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(48-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(48-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
6^{48}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_modular_arithmetic
Situation: power-remainder-cycle
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q224. The number \(1x658\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: smallest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1170\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Q230. Between \(199\) and \(318\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 199 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 318.

\[
318=15\times 21+3
\]

\[
318-3=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-210}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{318}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{198}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-13=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_large_expression_divisibility
Situation: checksum-number
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

Q234. With tens \(7\) and ones \(2\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(45\)
B. \(46\)
C. \(44\)
D. \(47\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+2=72
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+7=27
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-2|=9\times 5=45
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-2|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(72\)
B. \(70\)
C. \(73\)
D. \(71\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{64}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
37+18+9+4+2+1=71
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 75! is 71.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(71\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{32}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{64}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(71\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

C. \(13\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 167 is 171.

\[
9\times 19=171
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 289.

\[
289=9\times 32+1
\]

\[
289-1=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{288-171}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{289}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{166}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
32-18=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q250. When \(3^{76}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
3\rightarrow 0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(76-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
3^{76}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(76-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
3^{76}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_modular_cycle
Situation: range-remainder-count
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q258. Tens digit \(3\) and ones digit \(5\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+5=35
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 5+3=53
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-5|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-5|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 5 dividing 30! is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 239 is 240.

\[
6\times 40=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 364.

\[
364=6\times 60+4
\]

\[
364-4=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{360-240}{6}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{364}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{238}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
60-39=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q274. Power \(7^{65}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(65-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
7^{65}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(65-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{65}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q278. The number \(79x16\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 23; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: hidden-divisor-deduction
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

C. \(9\)

Answer: D

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
7-x=0
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7-7=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

B. \(7\)

D. \(6\)
Answer: C

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(41-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
11^{41}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(41-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
2^{41}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q302. The number \(7x901\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=17
\]

\[
17-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
17-x=11
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
17-6=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Situation: smallest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q319. When \(13^{35}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?

\[

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(35-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
13^{35}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

\[

\]

Q320. The number \(72x05\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=10
\]

\[
10+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
10+x=11
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
10+1=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q326. The number \(41x38\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: hidden-divisor-deduction
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q331. Between \(227\) and \(307\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 227 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 307.

\[
307=9\times 34+1
\]

\[
307-1=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{306-234}{9}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{307}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{226}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-25=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

D. \(2\)

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(93-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{93}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(93-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
3^{93}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q362. The number \(79x14\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: hidden-divisor-deduction
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q367. Between \(128\) and \(259\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 128 is 132.

\[
12\times 11=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 259.

\[
259=12\times 21+7
\]

\[
259-7=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{252-132}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{259}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{127}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-10=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: simultaneous-divisibility
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

Q369. Power \(3^{89}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(89-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
3^{89}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(89-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
3^{89}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_power
Situation: nested-remainder
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: elite

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=\frac{60\times 195}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,195)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(38\)
B. \(39\)
C. \(37\)
D. \(40\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
20+10+5+2+1=38
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 40! is 38.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(38\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(38\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

\[
p=(117-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
7^{117}\equiv 1\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q395. The number \(4x654\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: hidden-divisor-deduction
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Answer: C

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
12+2=14
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 60! = 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q401. Between \(205\) and \(316\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 205 is 207.

\[
9\times 23=207
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 316.

\[
316=9\times 35+1
\]

\[
316-1=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{315-207}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{316}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{204}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-22=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_large_expression_divisibility
Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(60-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
3^{60}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(60-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
3^{60}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q419. The number \(836x8\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=25
\]

\[
S+x=25+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
25+x=27
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
25+2=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q429. Between \(189\) and \(328\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 189 is 192.

\[
6\times 32=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 328.

\[
328=6\times 54+4
\]

\[
328-4=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{324-192}{6}+1=22+1=23
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 23.
Hence, there are 23 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{328}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{188}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
54-31=23
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q441. Between \(111\) and \(250\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 111 is 117.

\[
9\times 13=117
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 243 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 250.

\[
250=9\times 27+7
\]

\[
250-7=243
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{243-117}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{250}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{110}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
27-12=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: largest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: punjab_state

A. \(27\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+4=74
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+7=47
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-4|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-4|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q453. The number \(770x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q459. The number \(214x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Situation: hidden-divisor-deduction
Topology depth: 4

\]

Fast result: \(180\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q465. The number \(7x561\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
7-x=0
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7-7=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q470. Between \(222\) and \(344\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 222 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 344.

\[
344=15\times 22+14
\]

\[
344-14=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{330-225}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{344}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{221}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-14=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: largest-valid-digit
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: punjab_state

Q484. With tens \(3\) and ones \(1\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+1=31
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+3=13
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-1|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-1|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Topology depth: 5

Answer: D

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
16+5+1=22
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 50! is 22.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(22\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{9}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q486. For \(19^{100}\), use the cycle modulo \(17\). What remainder is left?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(100-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 16.

\[
19^{100}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 16.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(100-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

\[
2^{100}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q487. For two numbers \(165\) and \(105\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(1155\)
B. \(1271\)
C. \(1386\)
D. \(1039\)
Answer: A

\[
165=3\times 5\times 11
\]

\[
105=3\times 5\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,105)=1155
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1155.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1155\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,105)=\frac{165\times 105}{\operatorname{HCF}(165,105)}=1155
\]

Fast result: \(1155\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q492. The number \(192x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
Situation: elite-divisibility-chain
Topology depth: 5
Exam mode: elite
